=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a string s.
A string t is called good if all characters of t occur the same number of times.
You can perform the following operations any number of times:

Delete a character from s.
Insert a character in s.
Change a character in s to its next letter in the alphabet.

Note that you cannot change 'z' to 'a' using the third operation.
Return the minimum number of operations required to make s good.
 
Example 1:

Input: s = "acab"
Output: 1
Explanation:
We can make s good by deleting one occurrence of character 'a'.

Example 2:

Input: s = "wddw"
Output: 0
Explanation:
We do not need to perform any operations since s is initially good.

Example 3:

Input: s = "aaabc"
Output: 2
Explanation:
We can make s good by applying these operations:

Change one occurrence of 'a' to 'b'
Insert one occurrence of 'c' into s


 
Constraints:

3 <= s.length <= 2 * 10^4
s contains only lowercase English letters.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def makeStringGood(self, s: str) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Matching one unit saves 1 (because delete+insert cost2 vs change cost1). Surplus at i cannot match deficit at i (same node; if S_i and D_i both positive impossible). Cannot match deficit at i+2 without two changes cost2, no saving relative to delete+insert. So max saving is sum min(S_i,D_{i+1}). Achievable by moving min. Need ensure moving from i to i+1 doesn't interfere with other moves. Since S_i and D_{i+1} are independent; moving x_i reduces surplus at i and deficit at i+1. It doesn't affect S_{i+1} or D_i. It may affect ability of S_{i-1} to fill D_i? D_i is deficit at i; if i has deficit, S_i=0, so no outgoing x_i. If i has surplus, D_i=0, so no incoming needed. Thus no conflict. Wait what if i has S_i>0 and D_i=0, but also D_{i+1}>0; x_i uses S_i. What about S_{i-1} could fill D_i? D_i=0 no. Good. If i has D_i>0 and S_i=0, x_{i-1} fills D_i; x_i=0 because no surplus. So no conflict. Thus independent.

Now we need minimize over f_i ∈ {0,k} for k. Then S_i and D_i depend on k and p_i:
For each letter i:
- If p_i=0 (inactive): f_i=0. Then S_i = c_i, D_i=0.
- If p_i=1 (active): f_i=k. Then if c_i >= k: S_i = c_i - k, D_i=0. If c_i < k: S_i=0, D_i = k - c_i. If equal S=D=0.
So each letter state p determines either surplus or deficit:
Let c=cnt[i].
If p=0: S=c, D=0.
If p=1:
  S=max(c-k,0), D=max(k-c,0).

Then cost for a given p vector:
Cost = Σ_i (S_i + D_i) - Σ_{i=0}^{24} min(S_i, D_{i+1}).
Note D_0 cannot be filled from left, S_25 cannot be used. This formula only depends on adjacent pair (S_i, D_{i+1}). We need minimize over p_i ∈ {0,1} for each k. This is a binary Markov chain with 26 nodes and pair cost between i and i+1: -min(S_i(p_i), D_{i+1}(p_{i+1})). Unary cost S_i(p_i)+D_i(p_i). Need DP over p_i (0/1) O(26*2*2) per k. Great! This is likely the known solution.

Now need ensure DP for each k with p_i binary and cost formula yields min. Let's derive DP.

For fixed k, define for each i (0..25) two states:
state 0: p_i=0 (letter absent final)
state 1: p_i=1 (letter present final with count k)

Compute S_i[state], D_i[state]:
if state=0: S=cnt[i], D=0.
if state=1:
  if cnt[i] >= k: S=cnt[i]-k, D=0
  else: S=0, D=k-cnt[i]

Unary cost U_i[state] = S_i[state] + D_i[state].
Pair saving P_{i,i+1}[state_i][state_{i+1}] = min(S_i[state_i], D_{i+1}[state_{i+1}]) for i=0..24. Total cost = Σ U_i - Σ P_i.

Need minimize over states. This is a chain with 26 nodes. DP:
dp[state] for processed up to i, including unary cost up to i and pair savings up to edge i-1? Let's define.

Initialize at i=0: dp0[st] = U_0[st] (no previous edge).
For i from 1 to 25:
  new[st] = min over prev_st of dp[prev_st] + U_i[st] - min(S_{i-1}[prev_st], D_i[st])
At end answer for this k = min(dp[st] for st in 0,1). This includes all unary and savings edges 0..24. Note edge i-1 between i-1 and i. Good.

Need consider z cannot change to a; our edge i=24 y->z allowed; z has no outgoing so no edge 25. D_0 cannot be filled from left; DP starts no previous. S_25 unused in saving; but U includes S_25, deletion. Good.

class Solution:
    def makeStringGood(self, s: str) -> int:
        cnt = [0]*26
        for ch in s:
            cnt[ord(ch)-97] += 1
        maxc = max(cnt)
        ans = len(s)  # maybe n

            dp0 = [0,0]?

Need compute DP carefully.

For each k:
Initialize for i=0:
For state 0 (not keep):
  S = cnt[0]; D=0; U=S+D=cnt[0]
For state 1 (keep):
  if cnt[0] >= k: S=cnt[0]-k; D=0; U=cnt[0]-k
  else: S=0; D=k-cnt[0]; U=k-cnt[0]
dp = [U0, U1]  # after processing letter 0, no previous saving.

For i from 1 to 25:
  compute U0,U1 for current letter.
  compute S_prev for prev state? Need for saving min(S_prev, D_cur). S_prev depends on prev state and previous letter count. We can compute arrays prev_S = [S0, S1] for previous letter, cur_D = [D0,D1] for current letter. Also cur_U.
  new0 = min(
      dp[0] + U0 - min(prev_S[0], D0),
      dp[1] + U0 - min(prev_S[1], D0)
  )
  new1 = min(
      dp[0] + U1 - min(prev_S[0], D1),
      dp[1] + U1 - min(prev_S[1], D1)
  )
  dp = [new0,new1]
  prev_S = cur_S
At end ans = min(ans, min(dp)).

Now, let's think about the proof of cost formula in terms of S/D and min. We should be absolutely sure. Let's attempt a formal derivation.

Given final f_i. Let S_i = max(c_i - f_i,0), D_i = max(f_i - c_i,0). If we perform no changes, cost to delete all surplus and insert all deficits is A = Σ S_i + Σ D_i. A change operation from i to i+1 can reduce this cost by at most 1? Let's see: If we change one char from i to i+1, we reduce c_i by1 and increase c_{i+1} by1. In terms of S/D, how does A change?
- If originally S_i>0 and D_{i+1}>0, then reducing c_i by1 decreases S_i by1 (if S_i>0) and increasing c_{i+1} by1 decreases D_{i+1} by1 (if D_{i+1}>0), so A decreases by2. Cost of operation1, net saving1.
- If originally S_i>0 and c_{i+1} >= f_{i+1} (D_{i+1}=0), increasing c_{i+1} may increase S_{i+1} by1 or if c_{i+1}=f_{i+1} create surplus, A decreases by1 at i but increases by1 at i+1, net A unchanged; operation cost1, net worse.
- If originally D_i>0 and D_{i+1}>0? reducing c_i increases D_i by1, increasing c_{i+1} decreases D_{i+1} by1, A unchanged; operation cost1, worse.
- If S_i=0,D_i>0 and S_{i+1}>0? reducing c_i increases D_i, increasing c_{i+1} decreases S_{i+1}, A unchanged; worse.
Thus only beneficial changes are from a letter with surplus to immediate next letter with deficit. Each such change reduces A by2 and costs1, net saving1. Since changes only affect adjacent counts, and after performing a change, the surplus/deficit statuses update; but if we perform min(S_i,D_{i+1}) changes, each is beneficial. Could performing a beneficial change create new surplus/deficit that enables another beneficial change? Example S_i>0,D_{i+1}>0. After moving one from i to i+1, S_i decreases, D_{i+1} decreases. If S_i remains >0 and D_{i+1} remains >0, more changes beneficial. Up to min. It does not create S_{i+1} because D_{i+1}>0 means c_{i+1}<f_{i+1}; increasing c_{i+1} reduces deficit; as long as D_{i+1}>0, no surplus. It does not create D_i because S_i>0. So repeated beneficial changes are independent. Could a change from i to i+1 make i+1 have surplus if D_{i+1} < S_i and we move more than D_{i+1}? We shouldn't; optimal min. If move more, then i+1 surplus and A net? Let's see after filling deficit, further moves increase S_{i+1}, A decreases at i but increases at i+1, net A unchanged, cost1, worse. So no. Thus max net saving is Σ min(S_i,D_{i+1}). Achievable by moving min on each edge. Need ensure moving on edge i and edge i+1 don't conflict: Edge i uses S_i and D_{i+1}. Edge i+1 uses S_{i+1} and D_{i+2}. They are disjoint in terms of S/D? Edge i affects D_{i+1}; edge i+1 uses S_{i+1}; if D_{i+1}>0 then S_{i+1}=0, so edge i+1 has no surplus from i+1. If S_{i+1}>0 then D_{i+1}=0, so edge i has no deficit to fill. Thus at most one of edge i and i+1 can be active? Wait edge i active requires D_{i+1}>0, which implies S_{i+1}=0. Edge i+1 active requires S_{i+1}>0, which implies D_{i+1}=0. So they cannot both be active. Actually edge i and i+1 cannot both have positive min? Let's check: min(S_i,D_{i+1})>0 => D_{i+1}>0 => S_{i+1}=0. min(S_{i+1},D_{i+2})>0 => S_{i+1}>0. Contradiction. So adjacent edges cannot both have positive flow in optimal formula. Is that true? Example counts a=2,b=1,c=0, f a0,b2,c0: S_a=2,D_b=1, S_b? c_b=1<f_b=2 so S_b=0. Edge a-b active, edge b-c not. Yes. If S_b>0 then D_b=0, edge a-b not. So flows are non-adjacent. This makes conflict even less. But wait what about a letter with c_i > f_i (surplus) and c_{i+1} < f_{i+1} (deficit), then S_i>0,D_{i+1}>0. The next letter i+1 has deficit, so S_{i+1}=0. Thus no outgoing from i+1. So indeed changes cannot chain. This reinforces adjacent-only.

But is it possible that a letter has both S_i>0 and D_i>0? No by definition. Thus active edges alternate? If edge i active, node i has surplus, node i+1 has deficit. Node i+1 has no surplus, so edge i+1 inactive. Node i has no deficit, so edge i-1 inactive. Thus active edges are isolated. Nice.

This also means the DP pair saving min(S_i,D_{i+1}) is exact; no interaction between edges.

Now, let's maybe derive DP in code with arrays to avoid mistakes.

Pseudo:

```
class Solution:
    def makeStringGood(self, s: str) -> int:
        cnt = [0]*26
        for ch in s:
            cnt[ord(ch)-97] += 1
        maxc = max(cnt)
        ans = len(s)
        for k in range(1, maxc+1):
            # process letter 0
            # state 0: not keep
            S0_prev = cnt[0]
            D0_prev = 0
            U0_prev = cnt[0]
            # state 1: keep
            if cnt[0] >= k:
                S1_prev = cnt[0] - k
                D1_prev = 0
                U1_prev = cnt[0] - k
            else:
                S1_prev = 0
                D1_prev = k - cnt[0]
                U1_prev = k - cnt[0]
            dp = [U0_prev, U1_prev]
            prev_S = [S0_prev, S1_prev]
            for i in range(1, 26):
                c = cnt[i]
                # state 0
                U0 = c
                D0 = 0
                # state 1
                if c >= k:
                    U1 = c - k
                    S1 = c - k
                    D1 = 0
                else:
                    U1 = k - c
                    S1 = 0
                    D1 = k - c
                # current S for state0
                S0 = c
                # compute new dp
                ndp0 = min(
                    dp[0] + U0 - min(prev_S[0], D0),
                    dp[1] + U0 - min(prev_S[1], D0)
                )
                ndp1 = min(
                    dp[0] + U1 - min(prev_S[0], D1),
                    dp[1] + U1 - min(prev_S[1], D1)
                )
                dp = [ndp0, ndp1]
                prev_S = [S0, S1]
            ans = min(ans, min(dp))
        return ans
```

Need check for state0 current D0=0, so saving zero; ndp0 = min(dp)+U0. This means if current letter not kept, no incoming can be used; previous surplus must be deleted or used earlier? Wait what if previous surplus could be moved into current inactive letter and then current inactive letter's original chars moved to next? We argued not beneficial. But in DP, if current inactive, D0=0, so no saving from previous. However previous surplus may have been carried? Our model doesn't carry beyond one step; if current inactive, previous surplus cannot be used at current, so it must have been deleted at previous. But what if previous surplus could be moved to current inactive, and then current's original surplus moved to next, effectively moving previous surplus two steps equal to delete+insert. We ignore; min unaffected. Good.

But wait, what if current inactive has c_i>0 (S0=c_i), and previous active has surplus S_prev, and next active has deficit D_next. Could moving previous surplus into current and current's own surplus into next yield a cost lower than our formula? Let's test with numbers to be safe.

Example: counts a=1 (prev), b=1 (current inactive final0), c=0 (next active final1). Target k=1. f_a=0? Let's choose a inactive? Need previous surplus to move into b. Let's define letters 0,1,2. c0=1,c1=1,c2=0. f0=0,f1=0,f2=1. Our formula: S0=1,S1=1,D2=1. Savings: edge0 min(S0,D1=0)=0; edge1 min(S1,D2=1)=1. Cost ΣS=2,ΣD=1 -1=2. Manual optimal: delete a cost1, change b->c cost1 total2. Alternative: change a->b (cost1), change b->c (cost1), final b? Let's simulate: start a1,b1,c0. change a->b: a0,b2,c0. change b->c: a0,b1,c1. Need f b0, delete b cost1. Total3. Or change a->b, delete b? no. So formula good.

What if current inactive has c=0, previous surplus S0=1, next deficit D2=1. c0=1,c1=0,c2=0, f0=0,f1=0,f2=1. Formula S0=1,D2=1, no savings (D1=0,S1=0), cost2. Manual delete a insert c cost2; change a->b->c cost2 equal. Good.

What if current inactive has c=1, previous surplus S0=1, next deficit D2=2. c0=1,c1=1,c2=0, f0=0,f1=0,f2=2. Formula S0=1,S1=1,D2=2. Savings edge1 min(1,2)=1. Cost ΣS=2,ΣD=2-1=3. Can k>M ever be less than cost(M,p)? Let's derive. Let active set A. For k>M, all active have D_i = k-c_i, S_i=0. Inactive have S_i=c_i,D=0. Cost(k) = Σ_{i∉A} c_i + Σ_{i∈A}(k-c_i) - Σ_{i: i∉A, i+1∈A} min(c_i, k-c_{i+1}).

Difference cost(k)-cost(M) = |A|*(k-M) - Σ_{inactive->active} [min(c_i, k-c_{i+1}) - min(c_i, M-c_{i+1})].
The bracket is at most? If c_i <= M-c_{i+1}, then min(c_i,k-c_{i+1}) maybe c_i (if k-c_{i+1}>=c_i) so bracket0. If c_i > M-c_{i+1}, bracket ≤ k-M? Actually min(c_i, k-c_{i+1}) - min(c_i, M-c_{i+1}) ≤ (k-c_{i+1}) - (M-c_{i+1}) = k-M, but also ≤ c_i - (M-c_{i+1}) maybe. So each bracket ≤ k-M. There are at most |A| such edges (each active letter has at most one predecessor), so total saving increase ≤ |A|*(k-M). Thus cost(k) >= cost(M). Good. So k>M not better. Great.

Let's formalize the "only adjacent changes" proof:
Any sequence of operations can be transformed to one where each original character is changed at most once. If a character is changed from i to j with j>i, cost j-i. If j-i>=2, replace those changes by deleting the character at i and inserting a character at j; cost2 <= j-i. This may affect final counts? It results in same final multiset? Deleting at i and inserting at j changes counts by -1 at i and +1 at j, same as changing i to j. Cost no greater. If j-i=1, keep. Thus there exists optimal with only adjacent changes. This is key. Then our adjacent matching formula exact.

But wait, what if a character is changed from i to j and then later another operation changes it further? The total path length d. Replace entire path by delete+insert at final letter. If d>=2, cost2 <= d. If d=1, keep. If a character is inserted and then changed, replace by inserting at final letter cost1 <= 1+d? Actually insert at i then change to j cost1+d, insert at j cost1, better. So no inserted chars changed. If a character is changed and then deleted, delete at original cost1 <= d+1, better. So optimal has no changed-then-deleted, no inserted-then-changed, and no multi-step changes. Thus only adjacent changes of original chars that remain. Good.

Now, let's maybe compare our DP to min-cost flow for fixed f to ensure formula `ΣS+ΣD-Σmin(S_i,D_{i+1})` is indeed min for adjacent changes. Let's prove via marginal:
Starting from baseline where all surplus deleted and all deficits inserted, cost B=ΣS+ΣD. An adjacent change from i to i+1 can reduce B by:
- If it moves a unit that would otherwise be deleted at i (requires S_i>0) and supplies a unit that would otherwise be inserted at i+1 (requires D_{i+1}>0), B decreases by2, operation cost1, net saving1.
- Otherwise B does not decrease by2; operation cannot reduce total cost (may increase or keep). Since each unit moved can produce at most one net saving, and moving from i to i+1 only affects S_i and D_{i+1}, total net saving ≤ number of pairs (surplus at i, deficit at i+1) matched = min(S_i,D_{i+1}). Achievable by moving min. This is a bit handwavy but enough. Need ensure moving min on all edges simultaneously doesn't use same surplus/deficit twice. Since S_i and D_{i+1} are disjoint resources; each surplus at i can only match D_{i+1}; each deficit at i+1 can only match S_i. There is no other use. So sum min is achievable. Good.

But wait, could a surplus at i be used to fill deficit at i+1, and a surplus at i+1 be used to fill deficit at i+2, but if i+1 has deficit, it cannot have surplus. So no conflict. If i+1 has surplus, it has no deficit, so edge i not active. Thus edges active are independent. Good.

Now, let's think about if a letter can have both surplus and deficit due to changes? In final f, no. But after moving, a letter with surplus S_i sends x to next; if x < S_i, still surplus; if x = S_i, balanced. It never becomes deficit because x≤S_i. A letter with deficit D_i receives x from prev; if x < D_i, still deficit; if x = D_i, balanced. It never becomes surplus because x≤D_i. Thus statuses don't flip. This is important: by moving min, no letter changes from surplus to deficit or vice versa. So the matching is feasible and no cascading. Good.

Now, let's think about if moving min on edge i and edge i+1 could both be positive if letter i+1 has S=0 and D=0? Edge i requires D_{i+1}>0; edge i+1 requires S_{i+1}>0; impossible. So no letter both sends and receives. Thus operations are independent. Good.

Now, let's maybe write final code carefully:

```
class Solution:
    def makeStringGood(self, s: str) -> int:
        cnt = [0] * 26
        for ch in s:
            cnt[ord(ch) - 97] += 1

        max_cnt = max(cnt)
        ans = len(s)

        for k in range(1, max_cnt + 1):
            # letter 0
            c = cnt[0]
            # state 0: not keep
            dp0 = c
            prev_s0 = c
            # state 1: keep
            if c >= k:
                dp1 = c - k
                prev_s1 = c - k
            else:
                dp1 = k - c
                prev_s1 = 0

            dp = [dp0, dp1]
            prev_s = [prev_s0, prev_s1]

            for i in range(1, 26):
                c = cnt[i]

                # current state 0
                u0 = c
                d0 = 0
                s0 = c

                # current state 1
                if c >= k:
                    u1 = c - k
                    s1 = c - k
                    d1 = 0
                else:
                    u1 = k - c
                    s1 = 0
                    d1 = k - c

                ndp0 = min(
                    dp[0] + u0 - min(prev_s[0], d0),
                    dp[1] + u0 - min(prev_s[1], d0)
                )
                ndp1 = min(
                    dp[0] + u1 - min(prev_s[0], d1),
                    dp[1] + u1 - min(prev_s[1], d1)
                )

                dp = [ndp0, ndp1]
                prev_s = [s0, s1]

            ans = min(ans, min(dp))

        return ans
```

Since d0=0, ndp0 simplifies to min(dp)+u0. But keep general.

Potential bug: In initialization, for state1 if c < k, prev_s1=0, dp1=k-c. If c>=k, prev_s1=c-k, dp1=c-k. Good.

Potential bug: For current state0, s0=c. But if current inactive, it can send surplus to next; yes S=c. It cannot receive, D=0. Good.

Potential bug: For current state1, if c>=k, s1=c-k, d1=0; if c<k, s1=0,d1=k-c. Good.

Let's maybe try to derive known DP from our DP to see if there is any discrepancy with known accepted solutions. Suppose we define `dp[i][0]` = min cost for prefix up to i with letter i not kept, and `dp[i][1]` = kept. Our recurrence:
For current state 0:
`dp[i][0] = min(dp[i-1][0], dp[i-1][1]) + cnt[i]` because d0=0. This matches known.
For current state 1:
`dp[i][1] = min(
    dp[i-1][0] + U1 - min(S0_prev, D1),
    dp[i-1][1] + U1 - min(S1_prev, D1)
)`
where S0_prev = cnt[i-1] (if prev not kept), S1_prev = max(cnt[i-1]-k,0) (if prev kept), D1 = max(k-cnt[i],0), U1 = abs(cnt[i]-k) actually if cnt>=k U=cnt-k, if cnt<k U=k-cnt = abs.
If cnt[i] >= k, D1=0, so `dp[i][1] = min(dp[i-1]) + cnt[i]-k`. This matches known: if current kept and has enough, delete surplus.
If cnt[i] < k, D1=k-cnt[i], U1=k-cnt[i]. Then:
`dp[i][1] = min(
    dp[i-1][0] + (k-cnt[i]) - min(cnt[i-1], k-cnt[i]),
    dp[i-1][1] + (k-cnt[i]) - min(max(cnt[i-1]-k,0), k-cnt[i])
)`
Simplify first term: dp_prev0 + max(k-cnt[i] - cnt[i-1], 0)? Because D - min(cnt_prev,D) = max(D-cnt_prev,0). Second term: if cnt_prev>=k, S_prev=cnt_prev-k; D - min(S_prev,D)=max(D-S_prev,0); if cnt_prev<k, S_prev=0, term=D. This is more complex than known maybe. But okay.

Let's test known DP memory: I recall a solution:
```
dp[i][0] = min(dp[i-1][0], dp[i-1][1]) + cnt[i]
dp[i][1] = min(dp[i-1][0] + abs(cnt[i]-target),
               dp[i-1][1] + abs(cnt[i]-target) + min(cnt[i-1], target))
```
Wait that doesn't match. Let's search memory: There is LeetCode 3139 "Minimum Cost to Equalize Array"? No. Another problem "Minimum Number of Operations to Make String Good" maybe solution from leetcode:
```
cnt = [0]*26
for c in s: cnt[ord(c)-97]+=1
ans = n
for i in range(1, max(cnt)+1):
    dp = [[0,0] for _ in range(26)]
    dp[0][0] = cnt[0]
    dp[0][1] = abs(cnt[0]-i)
    for j in range(1,26):
        dp[j][0] = min(dp[j-1][0], dp[j-1][1]) + cnt[j]
        dp[j][1] = min(
            dp[j-1][0] + abs(cnt[j]-i),
            dp[j-1][1] + abs(cnt[j]-i) + min(cnt[j-1], i)
        )
    ans = min(ans, min(dp[25][0], dp[25][1]))
```
Let's test this known recurrence on example "aaabc" k=2:

dp0[0]=3, dp0[1]=1.
j=1 b:
dp1[0]=min(3,1)+1=2.
dp1[1]=min(
 dp0[0]+abs(1-2)=3+1=4,
 dp0[1]+abs(1-2)+min(cnt0=3,2)=1+1+2=4
) =4? But our DP for a,b active cost1. So known recurrence I recalled is wrong or for different problem. Maybe term should be `- min(cnt[j-1], i)`? Let's not rely on memory.

Before final, let's maybe attempt a formal proof of the DP cost formula to be absolutely certain. Suppose we have final counts f_i. Let S_i = max(c_i - f_i,0), D_i = max(f_i - c_i,0). Baseline cost B = ΣS_i + ΣD_i (delete all surplus, insert all deficits). Now consider performing adjacent changes. A change from i to i+1 can be beneficial only if it reduces both a deletion at i and an insertion at i+1. If we change a character that was not going to be deleted at i (i.e., c_i <= f_i), then we create a deficit at i requiring an insertion, so no net benefit. If we change a character to a letter that was not going to be inserted at i+1 (i.e., c_{i+1} >= f_{i+1}), then we create surplus at i+1 requiring deletion, no net benefit. Thus each beneficial adjacent change must pair one unit of S_i with one unit of D_{i+1}. Each such pair reduces B by2 and costs1, net saving1. Since each unit of S_i can be paired only with D_{i+1} (only adjacent to the right), and each unit of D_{i+1} only with S_i, the maximum number of such pairs is Σ min(S_i,D_{i+1}). Achievable by performing exactly min on each edge. Any other operation (multi-step, non-beneficial change) cannot reduce cost below this. Thus min cost = B - Σ min(S_i,D_{i+1}). This is solid.

Need ensure "each unit of S_i can be paired only with D_{i+1}" if multi-step changes could pair S_i with D_{i+2} at cost2, net saving? Baseline delete S_i cost1 + insert D_{i+2} cost1 =2; two changes cost2, net saving0. So no reduction. Thus not needed. Good.

Now, let's think about if a multi-step change from i to i+2 could pair S_i with D_{i+2} and also avoid deletion/insertion at i+1? It doesn't affect i+1 final if just passes through? Actually changing i->i+1 increases c_{i+1}; changing i+1->i+2 decreases c_{i+1}, net no change at i+1. It costs2 and reduces B by2 (S_i and D_{i+2}), net saving0. So no improvement. Good.

Now, let's think about if a change from i to i+1 where i+1 has D=0 but S_{i+1}>0, and then changing i+1 to i+2 could effectively move i's surplus to i+2 and i+1's surplus to i+2? Let's analyze: c_i surplus, c_{i+1} surplus, c_{i+2} deficit. Baseline cost S_i+S_{i+1}+D_{i+2}. Adjacent beneficial changes: only S_{i+1}->D_{i+2} saving1, cost = sum -1. Could multi-step move S_i to i+2 too? Change i->i+1 (cost1), then i+1 has extra surplus; change i+1->i+2 (cost1) for one unit. If we move S_i to i+2, cost2, reduces S_i and D_{i+2} by1, net saving0. So no lower. Good.

Let's maybe search mental snippets: A problem "Minimum Number of Operations to Make String Good" (LeetCode 3139?) had solution:
```
class Solution:
    def makeStringGood(self, s: str) -> int:
        n = len(s)
        cnt = [0]*26
        for c in s:
            cnt[ord(c)-97]+=1
        ans = n
        for i in range(1, max(cnt)+1):
            dp = [[0,0] for _ in range(26)]
            dp[0][0] = cnt[0]
            dp[0][1] = abs(cnt[0]-i)
            for j in range(1,26):
                dp[j][0] = min(dp[j-1][0], dp[j-1][1]) + cnt[j]
                dp[j][1] = min(
                    dp[j-1][0] + abs(cnt[j]-i),
                    dp[j-1][1] + abs(cnt[j]-i) + min(cnt[j-1], i)
                )
            ans = min(ans, min(dp[25]))
        return ans
```

Let's perhaps refine code to be slightly cleaner:

```
class Solution:
    def makeStringGood(self, s: str) -> int:
        cnt = [0] * 26
        for ch in s:
            cnt[ord(ch) - 97] += 1

        max_cnt = max(cnt)
        ans = len(s)

        for target in range(1, max_cnt + 1):
            # dp[0] = cost for processed prefix if current letter is absent
            # dp[1] = cost if current letter appears target times
            c = cnt[0]
            if c >= target:
                dp = [c, c - target]
                prev_surplus = [c, c - target]
            else:
                dp = [c, target - c]
                prev_surplus = [c, 0]

            for i in range(1, 26):
                c = cnt[i]

                # current letter absent
                cur_surplus0 = c
                cur_deficit0 = 0
                cost0 = c

                # current letter present
                if c >= target:
                    cur_surplus1 = c - target
                    cur_deficit1 = 0
                    cost1 = c - target
                else:
                    cur_surplus1 = 0
                    cur_deficit1 = target - c
                    cost1 = target - c

                ndp0 = min(
                    dp[0] + cost0 - min(prev_surplus[0], cur_deficit0),
                    dp[1] + cost0 - min(prev_surplus[1], cur_deficit0)
                )
                ndp1 = min(
                    dp[0] + cost1 - min(prev_surplus[0], cur_deficit1),
                    dp[1] + cost1 - min(prev_surplus[1], cur_deficit1)
                )

                dp = [ndp0, ndp1]
                prev_surplus = [cur_surplus0, cur_surplus1]

            ans = min(ans, min(dp))

        return ans
```

This is clear.

Potential issue: In initialization, if `cnt[0] >= target`, `dp = [c, c - target]`; `prev_surplus = [c, c - target]`. If `cnt[0] < target`, `dp
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
below.

```python
class Solution:
    def makeStringGood(self, s: str) -> int:
        cnt = [0] * 26
        for ch in s:
            cnt[ord(ch) - 97] += 1

        max_cnt = max(cnt)
        ans = len(s)

        for target in range(1, max_cnt + 1):
            c = cnt[0]

            # state 0: letter 0 is absent in the final string
            # state 1: letter 0 appears target times
            if c >= target:
                dp = [c, c - target]
                prev_surplus = [c, c - target]
            else:
                dp = [c, target - c]
                prev_surplus = [c, 0]

            for i in range(1, 26):
                c = cnt[i]

                # Current letter absent
                cur_surplus0 = c
                cur_deficit0 = 0
                cost0 = c

                # Current letter present
                if c >= target:
                    cur_surplus1 = c - target
                    cur_deficit1 = 0
                    cost1 = c - target
                else:
                    cur_surplus1 = 0
                    cur_deficit1 = target - c
                    cost1 = target - c

                ndp0 = min(
                    dp[0] + cost0 - min(prev_surplus[0], cur_deficit0),
                    dp[1] + cost0 - min(prev_surplus[1], cur_deficit0)
                )
                ndp1 = min(
                    dp[0] + cost1 - min(prev_surplus[0], cur_deficit1),
                    dp[1] + cost1 - min(prev_surplus[1], cur_deficit1)
                )

                dp = [ndp0, ndp1]
                prev_surplus = [cur_surplus0, cur_surplus1]

            ans = min(ans, min(dp))

        return ans
```
